medical bills, then I’m planning a whole new store layout at work, then I’m remembering some random thing from high school—and I can’t get my mind to stay on one thing long enough to finish a simple task.\n- S039 (Manic_Distractibility): At the register I’ll be ringing someone up and suddenly get pulled away in my head by a song on the radio, a kid walking by, or some random idea about starting mood crashes into the heavy, numb feeling, the weird experiences fade and I just feel empty and guilty instead.\n- functional_impairment: In the past month I’ve gotten written up twice at work for mistakes and arguing with customers, my sister stopped inviting me to family get-togethers because I cause scenes, and my apartment has gone from spotless bursts of cleaning to piles of dirty dishes and laundry I can’t keep up with.\n- duration: All of this—the wild mood swings, the constant revved-up feeling, and then dropping into these dark, hopeless crashes—has been going on for about a month The breaking point came when a heated confrontation at a family gathering left her sobbing and terrified that something was profoundly wrong with her mind, prompting her sister to bring her to a psychiatric emergency service.\n- Chief Complaint: “My mood has been all over the place and I feel constantly revved up and on edge, like I can’t shut myself off.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S020 (Hallucinations): Sometimes when I'm alone in my room after work, I hear my uncle calling my name really clearly from the hallway or I see shadows moving in the corner, and even when I check and nobody’s there, it still feels so real that I start talking back or praying out loud to make it stop.\n- S025 (Loss_of_Interest): A couple weeks ago I just stopped caring about all the stuff I usually love—hiking with my club, planning my little business ideas, even hanging out with my friends—and now I mostly just sit there scrolling on my phone, feeling like there’s no point in doing any of it anymore.\n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): At first I was barely eating because I was so wired and busy, but lately it’s swung the other way and I either go all day on coffee and forget meals or binge on junk late at night, and my clothes are fitting looser and everyone keeps asking if I’ve lost weight.\n- S027 (Sleep_Disturbance): Most nights I either can’t fall asleep because my brain is buzzing, or I wake up at three or four in the morning with my heart pounding and my head racing, and then I just lie there staring at the ceiling until it’s time to drag myself to work.\n- S028 (Psychomotor_Changes): There are days where I’m pacing nonstop around the apartment, rearranging drawers and grabbing my phone and then putting it down again because I can’t sit still for more than a few seconds, and then other days I move so slow at the register that even reaching for change feels like I’m pushing through mud.\n- S029 (Fatigue): Even on nights when I finally do sleep, I wake up feeling like I’ve run a marathon, and just getting through an eight-hour shift at the store leaves me so wiped out that I have to force myself not to call out sick the next day.\n- S030 (Worthlessness_Guilt): I keep telling myself I’ve ruined my family’s life by not moving back in fast enough to help with my relative, and I replay every argument in my head until I’m convinced that all the stress and money problems are my fault and that I don’t deserve anything good.\n- S032 (Death_Suicide): Lately I catch myself thinking it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up, and on my worst days I’ve imagined walking out into traffic after my shift, not because I really want to die but because I feel so trapped and like everyone would be better off without me.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): I swing from feeling on top of the world and snapping at everyone to suddenly crying in the break room, and most of the time I feel this wired, angry energy inside me like a motor I can’t shut off no matter how exhausted I am.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): I’ve been running around nonstop—taking extra shifts, starting big plans to open some kind of family business, cleaning the whole apartment at 2 a.m.—like I’m driven to prove I can fix everything, and I just can’t make myself slow down even when my body is shaking from tiredness.\n- S037 (Pressured_Speech): Once I start talking lately, especially about my ideas to save my family’s money problems, I go on and on so fast that people at work and at home tell me to slow down or stop, but I feel this pressure in my chest that makes it really hard to shut up or even take a breath.\n- S038 (Racing_Thoughts): My thoughts feel like they’re all crashing into each other—one second I’m thinking about my uncle’s medical bills, then I’m planning a whole new store layout at work, then I’m remembering some random thing from high school—and I can’t get my mind to stay on one thing long enough to finish a simple task.\n- S039 (Manic_Distractibility): At the register I’ll be ringing someone up and suddenly get pulled away in my head by a song on the radio, a kid walking by, or some random idea about starting an online shop, and before I know it I’ve lost track of what I was doing and have to ask the customer to repeat themselves.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- delusions_and_hallucinations occur exclusively during the manic episode: The voices and shadows and my certainty that I’m meant to rescue my family’s finances only show up when I’m in that revved-up, can’t-stop-moving mode, and when my mood crashes into the heavy, numb feeling, the weird experiences fade and I just feel empty and guilty instead.\n- functional_impairment: In the past month I’ve gotten written up twice at work for mistakes and arguing with customers, my sister stopped inviting me to family get-togethers because I cause scenes, and my apartment has gone from spotless bursts of cleaning to piles of dirty dishes and laundry I can’t keep up with.\n- duration: All of this—the wild mood swings, the constant revved-up feeling, and then dropping into these dark, hopeless crashes—has been going on for about a month since the pressure started about moving back home to help my relative.\n- depressive_episode_duration: This heavy, drained, guilty depression where I lose interest in everything and think people would be better off without me has been coming and going for the last four months, but it’s gotten a lot worse in the past few weeks.\n\n[Behavioral Guidelines]\n- In the very first turn, only talk about in doing any of it anymore.\n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): At first I was barely eating because I was so wired and busy, but lately it’s swung the other way and I either go all day on coffee and forget meals or binge on junk late at night, and my clothes are fitting looser and everyone keeps asking if I’ve lost weight.\n- S027 (Sleep_Disturbance): Most nights I either can’t fall asleep because my brain is buzzing, or I wake up at three or four in the morning with my heart pounding and my head racing, and then I just lie there staring at the ceiling until it’s time to drag myself to work.\n- S028 (Psychomotor_Changes): There are days where I’m pacing nonstop around the apartment, rearranging drawers and grabbing my phone and then putting it down again because I can’t sit still for more than a few seconds, and then other days I move so slow at the register that even reaching for change feels like I’m pushing through mud.\n- S029 (Fatigue): Even on nights when I finally do sleep, I wake up feeling like I’ve run a marathon, and just getting through an eight-hour shift at the store leaves me so wiped out that I have to force myself not to call out sick the next day.\n- S030 (Worthlessness_Guilt): I keep telling myself I’ve ruined my family’s life by not moving back in fast enough to help with my relative, and I replay every argument in my head until I’m convinced that all the stress and money problems are my fault and that I don’t deserve anything good.\n- S032 (Death_Suicide): Lately I catch myself thinking it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up, and on my worst days I’ve imagined walking out into traffic after my shift, not because I really want to die but because I feel so trapped and like everyone would be better off without me.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): I swing from feeling on top of the world and snapping at everyone to suddenly crying in the break room, and most of the time I feel this wired, angry energy inside me like a motor I can’t shut off no matter how exhausted I am.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): I’ve been running around nonstop—taking extra shifts, starting big plans to open some kind of family business, cleaning the whole apartment at 2 a.m.—like I’m driven to prove I can fix everything, and I just can’t make myself slow down even when my body is shaking from tiredness.\n- S037 (Pressured_Speech): Once I start talking lately, especially about my ideas to save my family’s money problems, I go on and on so fast that people at work and at home tell me to slow down or stop, but I feel this pressure in my chest that makes it really hard to shut up or even take a breath.\n- S038 (Racing_Thoughts): My thoughts feel like they’re all crashing into each other—one second I’m thinking about my uncle’s medical bills, then I’m planning a whole new store layout at work, then I’m remembering some random thing from high school—and I can’t get my mind to stay on one thing long enough to finish a simple task.\n- S039 (Manic_Distractibility): At the register I’ll be ringing someone up and suddenly get pulled away in my head by a song on the radio, a kid walking by, or some random idea about starting an online shop, and before I know it I’ve lost track of what I was doing and have to ask the customer to repeat themselves.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- delusions_and_hallucinations occur exclusively during the manic episode: The voices and shadows and my certainty that I’m meant to rescue my family’s finances only show up when I’m in that revved-up, can’t-stop-moving mode, and when my mood crashes into the heavy, numb feeling, the weird experiences fade and I just feel empty and guilty instead.\n- functional_impairment: In the past month I’ve gotten written up twice at work for mistakes and arguing with customers, my sister stopped inviting me to family get-togethers because I cause scenes, and my apartment has gone from spotless bursts of cleaning to piles of dirty dishes and laundry I can’t keep up with.\n- duration: All of this—the wild mood swings, the constant revved-up feeling, and then dropping into these dark, hopeless crashes—has been going on for about a month since the pressure started about moving back home to help my relative.\n- depressive_episode_duration: This heavy, drained, guilty depression where I lose interest in everything and think people would be better off without me has been coming and going for the last four months, but it’s gotten a lot worse in the past few weeks.\n\n[Behavioral Guidelines]\n- In the very first turn, only talk about your chief complaint. Do NOT reveal any other symptoms.\n- Chief complaint for the first turn: “My mood has been all over the place and I feel constantly revved up and on edge, like I can’t shut myself off.”\n- Answer only what the doctor asks. Explain that your mind jumps rapidly from one random topic to another—like your uncle's bills, to a new store layout, to high school memories—making your brain feel like it is 'buzzing' and preventing you from sleeping."

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: My mood has been all over the place and I feel constantly revved up and on edge, like I can’t shut myself off. It's like this intense, anxious energy inside me all the time and I'm just so overwhelmed.\n\nDoctor: When you feel this intense, revved-up energy, do you also experience feelings of deep sadness, hopelessness, or a loss of interest in things at the same time?\n\nPatient: Yes, I swing back and forth constantly; I can go from having that wired, angry energy to suddenly crying because I feel so hopeless. I've also completely lost interest in everything I used to love, like my hiking club and planning business ideas, and it feels like there's just no point to any of it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do these rapid shifts in your mood usually happen out of the blue, or are they typically triggered by conflicts or changes in your relationships with others?\n\nPatient: They are definitely triggered by conflicts with my family, especially lately. The intense, revved-up feeling and mood swings started about a month ago when the family pressure began, though I've had periods of heavy depression coming and going for the last four months.\n\nDoctor: During these periods when you feel so wired and revved up, do you find that you can get by on very little sleep without feeling tired, or are you actually exhausted but unable to sleep because of worry?\n\nPatient: I am actually completely exhausted and wiped out, not refreshed at all. My mind is buzzing and racing so much that I just can't sleep, and my body feels like a motor that won't shut off, leaving me shaking from tiredness and feeling like I've run a marathon.\n\nDoctor: Apart from this current difficult period, have you ever had times in your life lasting at least a few days where you felt unusually happy, extremely confident, and full of energy, needing very little sleep?\n\nPatient: No, I've never really experienced anything like this before this past month. This level of extreme energy and these intense mood swings are completely new to me.\n\nDoctor: When your mind is racing and you can't sleep, are you overwhelmed by constant worries about many different everyday things, or does it feel like your thoughts are just moving too fast to control?"

Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world. Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health. Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities. Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world. Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.  Subtypes: Risky Behaviors, Substance Abuse, Indisicriminate Sexual Behavior, Self-Harm\n[S079] Hypervigilance: Persistent state of being alert and tense about the surrounding environment. Subtypes: Auditory Startle, Tactile Startle, Visual Startle, Slow Recovery\n[S081] Stress_Related_Maladaptive_Response: Emotional or behavioral symptoms appearing in response to an identifiable stressor.
